muse-spark-1.3-contributor (high) returned NOT READY. All material findings
//! Database-authoritative release history and explicit dormant pruning.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#4-explicit-pruning-and-audit — no implicit collector or unaudited data loss.
use super::*;
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct DormantItem {
    /// Stable cursor; column/index names may coincide.
    pub key: String,
    pub path: String,
    pub kind: String,
    pub action: String,
    pub rows: u64,
    pub bytes: u64,
    pub asset_bytes: u64,
    /// Internal audit distinction: zero standalone cells may still be covered by a dormant row.
    #[serde(skip)]
    pub(crate) definition_only: bool,
    pub sample_ids: Vec<String>,
    #[serde(default,skip_serializing_if="Option::is_none")]
    pub before_rows:Option<u64>,
    #[serde(default,skip_serializing_if="Option::is_none")]
    pub after_rows:Option<u64>,
    #[serde(default,skip_serializing_if="Option::is_none")]
    pub restored_rows:Option<u64>,
    #[serde(default,skip_serializing_if="Option::is_none")]
    pub before_sample_ids:Option<Vec<String>>,
}

impl DormantItem {
    pub fn unit(&self) -> &'static str {
        match (self.kind.as_str(), self.rows) { ("column", 1) => "cell", ("column", _) => "cells", ("index", 1) => "entry", ("index", _) => "entries", (_, 1) => "row", _ => "rows" }
    }
}

impl Store {
    /// Values presently in active rows, independently of table population or other columns.
    pub fn rollback_incompatible_values(&self, current: &Schema, target: &Schema, name: &str, column: &str) -> Result<(u64, Vec<String>), RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.authority_transaction()?;
        let table = &target.tables[name];
        let kind = &table.columns[column];
        if table.engine_field(column) || table.maintained.contains_key(column) { return Ok((0, Vec::new())); }
        let mut count = 0;
        let mut samples = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
        let mut statement = tx.conn().prepare("SELECT data FROM r WHERE t=(SELECT num FROM _catalog WHERE name=?1) ORDER BY id")?;
        for data in statement.query_map([name], |r| r.get::<_, String>(0))? {
            let mut row: Value = serde_json::from_str(&data?).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
            crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, &current.tables[name]);
            if let Ok(value) = row.field(column)
                && super::narrowed::incompatible_column(&tx, table, name, column, kind, value)? {
                count += 1;
                if samples.len() < 5 {
                    let text = match value { Value::String(s) => format!("'{}'", s.replace('\\', "\\\\").replace('\'', "\\'").replace('\n', "\\n").replace('\r', "\\r")), _ => canonical_json(value) };
                    samples.insert(super::adopt_refusals::bounded(&text));
                }
            }
        }
        Ok((count, samples.into_iter().collect()))
    }
}

fn sql_number(n: u64) -> Result<i64, RuntimeError> {
    i64::try_from(n).map_err(|_| {
        RuntimeError::Generation("release counter exceeds SQLite integer range".into())
    })
}

pub(super) fn present(conn: &Connection) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    Ok(conn.query_row(
        "SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE type='table' AND name='_dormant_tables')",
        [],
        |r| r.get(0),
    )?)
}
pub(crate) fn inventory(
    conn: &Connection,
    after: Option<&str>,
    limit: usize,
) -> Result<Vec<DormantItem>, RuntimeError> {
    if !present(conn)? {
        return Ok(Vec::new());
    }
    let narrowed = if super::narrowed::present(conn)? {
        " UNION ALL SELECT c.name,'rows',c.name,NULL FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=n.t GROUP BY n.t "
    } else {""};
    let query=format!("SELECT path,kind,table_name,column_name FROM (
        SELECT table_name AS path,'table' AS kind,table_name,NULL AS column_name FROM _dormant_tables
        UNION ALL SELECT table_name||'.'||column_name,'column',table_name,column_name FROM _dormant_columns c WHERE table_name NOT IN (SELECT table_name FROM _dormant_tables) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values v JOIN _catalog t ON t.num=v.t WHERE t.name=c.table_name AND v.column_name=c.column_name)
        UNION ALL SELECT table_name||'.'||index_name,'index',table_name,index_name FROM _dormant_indexes {narrowed})
        WHERE (?1 IS NULL OR kind||':'||path>?1) ORDER BY kind||':'||path LIMIT ?2");
    let mut statement=conn.prepare(&query)?;
    let items: Vec<(String, String, String, Option<String>)> = statement
        .query_map(
            params![after, i64::try_from(limit).unwrap_or(i64::MAX)],
            |r| Ok((r.get(0)?, r.get(1)?, r.get(2)?, r.get(3)?)),
        )?
        .collect::<Result<_, _>>()?;
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    for (path, kind, name, column) in items {
        let t: Option<i64> = conn
            .query_row("SELECT num FROM _catalog WHERE name=?1", [&name], |r| {
                r.get(0)
            })
            .optional()?;
        let Some(t) = t else { continue };
        // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#11-wave-11-factual-release-narration — dormant row images cover their retained cells; standalone cells have active owners.
        let (mut count,mut bytes):(u64,u64)=match kind.as_str(){
            "table"=>conn.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*),COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(id)+octet_length(data)),0) FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1",[t],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,i64>(0)? as u64,r.get::<_,i64>(1)? as u64)))?,
            "rows"=>conn.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*),COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(id)+octet_length(data)),0) FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1",[t],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,i64>(0)? as u64,r.get::<_,i64>(1)? as u64)))?,
            "column"=>conn.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*),COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(id)+octet_length(data)),0) FROM _dormant_values v WHERE t=?1 AND column_name=?2 AND EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=v.t AND r.id=v.id)",params![t,column],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,i64>(0)? as u64,r.get::<_,i64>(1)? as u64)))?,
            _=>conn.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*),0 FROM (SELECT id FROM r WHERE t=?1 UNION SELECT id FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1)",[t],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,i64>(0)? as u64,r.get::<_,i64>(1)? as u64)))?,
        };
        if matches!(kind.as_str(), "table" | "rows") {
            let images = if kind == "table" { "_dormant_rows" } else { "_dormant_narrow_rows" };
            bytes += conn.query_row(&format!("SELECT COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(v.id)+octet_length(v.data)),0) FROM _dormant_values v WHERE v.t=?1 AND EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM {images} d WHERE d.t=v.t AND d.id=v.id)"), [t], |r| r.get::<_, i64>(0))? as u64;
        }
        let query = match kind.as_str() {
            "rows" => "SELECT id FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT 5",
            "table" => "SELECT id FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT 5",
            "column" => {
                "SELECT id FROM _dormant_values v WHERE t=?1 AND column_name=?2 AND EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=v.t AND r.id=v.id) ORDER BY id LIMIT 5"
            }
            _ => {
                "SELECT id FROM (SELECT id FROM r WHERE t=?1 UNION SELECT id FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1) WHERE ?2 IS NOT NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT 5"
            }
        };
        let mut ids = conn
            .prepare(query)?
            .query_map(params![t, column], |r| r.get::<_, String>(0))?
            .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?
            .into_iter()
            .map(|id| super::adopt_refusals::bounded(&id))
            .collect();
        if kind == "index" {
            (count, ids) = index_entries(conn, t, &name, column.as_deref().unwrap())?;
        }
        let asset_bytes = if kind == "index" {
            0
        } else {
            let selector=match kind.as_str(){
                "rows"=>"EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n WHERE n.t=?3 AND n.id=_assets.home_id)",
                "column"=>"EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values v JOIN r ON r.t=v.t AND r.id=v.id WHERE v.t=?3 AND v.id=_assets.home_id AND v.column_name=_assets.home_column)",
                _=>"EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_rows d WHERE d.t=?3 AND d.id=_assets.home_id)",
            };
            conn.query_row(&format!("SELECT COALESCE(SUM(bytes),0) FROM _assets WHERE home_table=?1 AND (?2 IS NULL OR home_column=?2) AND released_at IS NULL AND {selector}"),params![name,if kind=="table" || kind=="rows" {None}else{column.as_deref()},t],|r|r.get::<_,i64>(0).map(|n|n as u64))?
        };
        let definition_only = if kind == "column" {
            !conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND column_name=?2)", params![t, column], |r| r.get::<_, bool>(0))?
        } else { count == 0 };
        out.push(DormantItem {
            key: format!("{kind}:{path}"),
            path,
            kind,
            action: "dormant".into(),
            rows: count,
            bytes,
            asset_bytes,
            definition_only,
            sample_ids: ids,
            before_rows:None,after_rows:None,restored_rows:None,before_sample_ids:None,
        });
    }
    Ok(out)
}

/// Retained definitions represent logical entries, rebuilt on restoration.
/// A words index has zero or many entries per image; a row count is not its size.
fn index_entries(conn: &Connection, t: i64, table: &str, index: &str) -> Result<(u64, Vec<String>), RuntimeError> {
    let definition: String = conn.query_row("SELECT definition FROM _dormant_indexes WHERE table_name=?1 AND index_name=?2", params![table, index], |r| r.get(0))?;
    let index: crate::schema::Index = serde_json::from_str(&definition).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
    let schema: Option<String> = conn.query_row("SELECT schema FROM _generations WHERE generation=(SELECT CAST(value AS INTEGER) FROM _meta WHERE key='generation')", [], |r| r.get(0)).optional()?;
    let schema: Option<Schema> = schema.map(|s| serde_json::from_str(&s).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))).transpose()?;
    let query = if super::narrowed::present(conn)? {
        "SELECT id,data FROM r WHERE t=?1 UNION ALL SELECT id,data FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1 UNION ALL SELECT id,data FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 ORDER BY id"
    } else { "SELECT id,data FROM r WHERE t=?1 UNION ALL SELECT id,data FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1 ORDER BY id" };
    let mut count = 0;
    let mut samples = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    let mut statement = conn.prepare(query)?;
    for image in statement.query_map([t], |r| Ok((r.get::<_, String>(0)?, r.get::<_, String>(1)?)))? {
        let (id, data) = image?;
        let mut row: Value = serde_json::from_str(&data).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
        if let Some(table) = schema.as_ref().and_then(|s| s.table(table)) { crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table); }
        for cell in conn.prepare("SELECT column_name,data FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2")?.query_map(params![t, id], |r| Ok((r.get::<_, String>(0)?, r.get::<_, String>(1)?)))? {
            let (column, value) = cell?;
            if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut row {
                fields.entry(column).or_insert(serde_json::from_str(&value).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?);
            }
        }
        let entries = crate::rows::index_keys(&index, &row, &id).len() as u64;
        count += entries;
        if entries > 0 && samples.len() < 5 { samples.insert(super::adopt_refusals::bounded(&id)); }
    }
    Ok((count, samples.into_iter().collect()))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#91-per-row-narrowed-value-dormancy — only rows surviving every activation phase count as restored.
pub(crate) fn finalize_restorations(tx:&Tx<'_>) -> Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    let entries=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT key,value FROM _meta WHERE key LIKE 'activation_narrowed_restored_ids:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_column_restored_ids:%' OR key LIKE 'activation_table_restored_ids:%'")?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get::<_,String>(0)?,r.get::<_,String>(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<Vec<_>,_>>()?;
    let mut narrowed=0u64;
    for (key,ids) in entries {
        let (prefix,path)=key.split_once(':').expect("activation restoration key");
        let table=if prefix=="activation_column_restored_ids" {path.split_once('.').expect("column path").0} else {path};
        let count:u64=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id IN (SELECT value FROM json_each(?2))",params![tx.num(table)?,ids],|r|r.get::<_,i64>(0).map(|n|n as u64))?;
        tx.set_meta(&format!("{}:{path}",prefix.trim_end_matches("_ids")),&count.to_string())?;
        if prefix=="activation_narrowed_restored_ids" {narrowed+=count;}
    }
    tx.set_meta("activation_narrowed_restored",&narrowed.to_string())?;
    Ok(())
}

pub(crate) fn event(
    tx: &Tx<'_>,
    operation: &str,
    release: Option<u64>,
    previous: Option<u64>,
    summary: serde_json::Value,
    items: &[DormantItem],
) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
    let revision = tx
        .meta("release_revision")?
        .and_then(|v| v.parse::<u64>().ok())
        .unwrap_or(0)
        .checked_add(1)
        .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Generation("release revision exhausted".into()))?;
    tx.set_meta("release_revision", &revision.to_string())?;
    let generation = tx
        .meta("generation")?
        .and_then(|v| v.parse::<u64>().ok())
        .unwrap_or(1);
    tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _release_events(at,operation,release,previous,generation,revision,summary) VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5,?6,?7)",params![crate::now_ms(),operation,release.map(sql_number).transpose()?,previous.map(sql_number).transpose()?,sql_number(generation)?,sql_number(revision)?,summary.to_string()])?;
    let id = tx.conn().last_insert_rowid();
    for item in items {
        tx.conn().execute(
            "INSERT INTO _release_event_items VALUES(?1,?2,?3)",
            params![id, item.key, canonical_json(item)],
        )?;
    }
    Ok(revision)
}

impl Store {
    pub fn dormant_inventory(
        &mut self,
        after: Option<&str>,
        limit: usize,
    ) -> Result<Vec<DormantItem>, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.read_transaction()?;
        let items = inventory(tx.conn(), after, limit)?;
        tx.rollback()?;
        Ok(items)
    }
    /// One snapshot: current identity, bounded history and storage inventories.
    pub fn release_status(
        &mut self,
        after: u64,
        items_after: Option<&str>,
        event_id: Option<u64>,
    ) -> Result<serde_json::Value, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.read_transaction()?;
        let mut events = Vec::new();
        let mut inventory_page = Vec::<serde_json::Value>::new();
        if present(tx.conn())? {
            let mut s=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT id,at,operation,release,previous,generation,revision,summary FROM _release_events WHERE id>?1 ORDER BY id LIMIT 16")?;
            let mut rows = s.query([sql_number(after)?])?;
            while let Some(r) = rows.next()? {
                events.push(serde_json::json!({"id":r.get::<_,i64>(0)?,"at":r.get::<_,i64>(1)?,"operation":r.get::<_,String>(2)?,"release":r.get::<_,Option<i64>>(3)?,"previous":r.get::<_,Option<i64>>(4)?,"generation":r.get::<_,i64>(5)?,"revision":r.get::<_,i64>(6)?,"summary":serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(&r.get::<_,String>(7)?).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?}));
            }
            if let Some(event) = event_id {
                let mut s=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT detail FROM _release_event_items WHERE event=?1 AND (?2 IS NULL OR path>?2) ORDER BY path LIMIT 16")?;
                for item in s.query_map(params![sql_number(event)?, items_after], |r| {
                    r.get::<_, String>(0)
                })? {
                    inventory_page.push(
                        serde_json::from_str(&item?)
                            .map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?,
                    );
                }
            } else {
                inventory_page = inventory(tx.conn(), items_after, 16)?
                    .into_iter()
                    .map(|i| serde_json::to_value(i).unwrap())
                    .collect();
            }
        }
        let workers:Vec<(String,String)>=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT key,value FROM _meta WHERE key='worker:status' OR key LIKE 'worker:status:%' ORDER BY key LIMIT 16")?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
        let previous_active: Option<u64> = if present(tx.conn())? {tx.conn().query_row("SELECT previous FROM _release_events WHERE release IS NOT NULL AND previous != release AND operation != 'prune' ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1",[],|r|r.get::<_,Option<i64>>(0)).optional()?.flatten().map(|n|n as u64)} else {None};
        let result = serde_json::json!({"previous_active":previous_active,"release":tx.meta("release")?.and_then(|v|v.parse::<u64>().ok()),"revision":tx.meta("release_revision")?.and_then(|v|v.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0),"generation":tx.meta("generation")?.and_then(|v|v.parse::<u64>().ok()).unwrap_or(1),"native_digest":tx.meta("release_native_digest")?,"events_after":events.last().map(|e|e["id"].clone()),"events":events,"items_after":inventory_page.last().map(|i|i["key"].clone()),"items":inventory_page,"event":event_id,"workers":workers});
        tx.rollback()?;
        Ok(result)
    }
    pub fn prune_dormant(
        &mut self,
        confirm: bool,
        expected_revision: Option<u64>,
    ) -> Result<serde_json::Value, RuntimeError> {
        let tx = self.transaction()?;
        let held = tx
            .meta("release_revision")?
            .and_then(|v| v.parse::<u64>().ok())
            .unwrap_or(0);
        if expected_revision.is_some_and(|n| n != held) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation(
                "release revision changed; run releases and retry prune".into(),
            ));
        }
        let items = inventory(tx.conn(), None, usize::MAX)?;
        if !confirm {
            tx.rollback()?;
            return Ok(
                serde_json::json!({"pruned":false,"items":items,"rewrite":"snapback4 prune <dir> --allow-destructive"}),
            );
        }
        if !present(tx.conn())? {
            tx.rollback()?;
            return Ok(serde_json::json!({"pruned":true,"items":[],"revision":held}));
        }
        // Release only dormant homes; active application data is untouched.
        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _assets SET released_at=COALESCE(released_at,?1) WHERE home_table IN (SELECT table_name FROM _dormant_tables) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values v JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=v.t WHERE c.name=_assets.home_table AND v.column_name=_assets.home_column AND v.id=_assets.home_id)",[crate::now_ms()])?;
        if super::narrowed::present(tx.conn())? {
            tx.conn().execute("UPDATE _assets SET released_at=COALESCE(released_at,?1) WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n JOIN _catalog c ON c.num=n.t WHERE c.name=_assets.home_table AND n.id=_assets.home_id)",[crate::now_ms()])?;
            tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _dormant_values WHERE EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n WHERE n.t=_dormant_values.t AND n.id=_dormant_values.id)",[])?;
            tx.conn().execute("DELETE FROM _dormant_narrow_rows",[])?;
        }
        let revision = event(
            &tx,
            "prune",
            tx.meta("release")?.and_then(|v| v.parse().ok()),
            None,
            serde_json::json!({"dropped_items":items.len(),"bytes":items.iter().map(|i|i.bytes).sum::<u64>(),"asset_bytes":items.iter().map(|i|i.asset_bytes).sum::<u64>()}),
            &items,
        )?;
        let event_id = tx.conn().last_insert_rowid();
        tx.conn().execute_batch("DELETE FROM _dormant_values;DELETE FROM _dormant_rows;DELETE FROM _dormant_refs;DELETE FROM _dormant_columns;DELETE FROM _dormant_indexes;DELETE FROM _dormant_tables;")?;
        tx.commit()?;
        Ok(serde_json::json!({"pruned":true,"items":items,"revision":revision,"event":event_id}))
    }
}

Scheduled behavior compares the schedule definition and lexical authored job
source, excluding comments, whitespace and source positions, before comparing
compiled programs. Missing job/schedule, edited schedule and edited job body
are distinct warnings. An unchanged body whose compiled program changes gets
a dependency note naming changed read/written tables and added/removed columns
when available. Missing saved source does not justify claiming a job absent.
Rollback preview also lists operations absent in the candidate and current
rule clauses differing from its rule. These are behavior differences to review,
not claims that every changed rule weakens enforcement. Removed table populations
are named without guessing whether a generic table is a hold/safeguard.

Receipts report total current dormant rows (whole-table and narrowed), cells
and logical index entries separately, and narrowed restorations separately.
Samples are sorted, de-duplicated and labelled by kind/path, at most five ids
per path. FIX28 makes row and cell populations disjoint: whole-table/narrowed dormant
rows include any separately retained cells of those same ids. Column counts and
samples include only retained cells of still-active rows. Covered cell bytes and
assets belong to their dormant row item; definitions with zero standalone cells
remain inventoried for restoration. Index entries are an independent logical
unit. The archived-row rollback example is one row and two cells, with disjoint
sample ids. Restoration counters still record actual consumed cells, including
covered cells, independently of the current inventory counts.
Per-column preview steps use those same standalone cell counts. A removed
column with zero standalone cells says no standalone cells become dormant,
explaining that cells of any dormant rows count with their rows; it never says
"its cells become dormant (0 cells)". The many-column enum-narrowing fixture
has one incompatible row and no active rows: its correct plan is one row and
zero cells. Adding a compatible row yields one row and twenty standalone cells,
one per removed column, with only the compatible row in each column's samples.

the entries those definitions represent over current/retained images, including
word-token fanout and dormant cells, rather than labelling row population as
entries. The existing inventory JSON field `rows` retains its count slot;
`kind` determines its unit. Human column/index previews use cells/entries.

release-control drains, while retaining its ownership sidecar and lock. Owner

Dead-owner reclaim is silent normally and a note under --verbose, without an
old URL that could be mistaken for new readiness. Busy live ownership still

## 9. Wave 9 release lifecycle amendment

Implementer: REL, 2026-10-12. This supersedes §3's narrowing refusal and §5's exposed request-generation race for release serving. HAND owns device version negotiation; DX owns production-writer diagnostics and starter/password UI.

### 9.1 Per-row narrowed-value dormancy

Each actual release activation, including roll-forward and equal-schema/program-only/native-only selection, runs the row-visibility closure. A visibility change advances generation even if execution hashes are equal, so devices replace their population; identical-source deploy no-ops do not activate. The closure removes authoritative rows whose **present** values do not fit the candidate column type or text constraint (asset leaves include ledger-backed kind, byte bound and file MIME allow-list, recursively through optionals/lists) into `_dormant_narrow_rows(t,id,data,definition,reason)`. Store format advances to `snapback4-release-store-3`; this binary opens format 2 and upgrades only during committed activation. Absent new required fields remain E_ADOPT. Engine metadata and maintained cache fields are not a narrowing trigger. Store the full authoritative image and its source table definition (merge restored whole-table and column definitions before classifying restored values), with interpreted old literal defaults and nulls, before projection. Restore any retained image that fits the candidate, splitting omitted columns into existing dormant cells. Restoration is automatic even on deploy; this is an existing row lifetime, not reuse of a removed schema name. No fallback syntax ships: the declared policy is hidden rows, never value substitution.

Visibility is a fixed point: a row with a non-null reference (including nested/list references) to a dormant narrowed row is also dormant. This covers authorization parents and memberships. Active programs, rules, point reads, lists, counts, caps, maintained views, assets and application SQL see only active r/i. Tentative restore-and-rehide work does not count as a visibility change; compute population change from newly hidden rows and successfully restored rows in the final closed population. All dependent physical indexes and aggregates rebuild when rows enter or leave dormancy, including otherwise unchanged tables. Sync generation replacement clears base/index images and acquisition, then reacquires only that same active population. This supersedes the device literal-default image-retention optimization: an additive default can restore a dormant cell whose actual value is incompatible, so schema shape alone cannot certify an old image. Same-generation attachment and identical-source no-ops preserve images; outbox custody survives generation replacement. HAND owns the shipped-device compatibility floor; DX owns updates to client/evolution guide text. A reference lookup to a dormant id is absent. Preserve LLP 3000.039 §1/§4.4: references validate a nominal id, not target existence. A new write may carry a dormant id just as it may carry an unknown/hidden id; read-through or explicit require/insert rules decide target admission. Such a write remains active under the old release until the next adoption's reference-closure pass, but grants no parent-derived authority. Never probe dormancy selectively at the reference write boundary, which would be an existence oracle. Dormant rows contribute neither to uniqueness nor caps. Restoration uses the same set-null cap exemption as ordinary writes; null-exempt groups are never counted by adoption or successor assessment. Their ids remain reserved: inserts cannot replace them and return the same E_RULE as a hidden live identity conflict, without row contents or a special dormant code. Updates/deletes by old programs see an absent row; active updates retain unrelated dormant cells. Dormant uniqueness keys are not reserved: old writes may cause a constraint conflict on forward, which E_ADOPT reports with executable application SQL; resolve that conflict under the current release when those rows are active. If both conflict rows are dormant, deploy a bridge with compatible widened types and the conflicting index non-unique/cap increased using --restore-dormant, restore both, repair with authorized mutations, then reinstate the constraint. Neither row silently wins; activation refuses atomically. Diagnostics include this bridge and application SQL, with values only in privileged operator output (§3). Type incompatibility alone never blocks switching.

At one pinned activation time, retire affected expired predecessor read-through rows under their old deadlines before narrowed hiding; retirement uses the ordinary authoritative delete to invalidate dependent evidence and remove dormant cells. Then restore candidate-compatible images, hide the incompatible/reference-dependent population, rebuild all physical r/i and _cap_counts/maintained caches, and validate before publication. Counts, sums and private parent-column mirrors all use this order. Reference closure removes at least one active row per iteration, terminates on the finite store even with nominal cycles, and any non-null element of a nested/list reference can hide its row. Null has no edge; a missing ordinary nominal target alone does not trigger hiding, while an absent target schema or narrowed-dormant target does. Capture dormant reference lifetime evidence before hiding; hiding is not deletion. Actual parent deletion invalidates evidence. Transferring a retained whole row or cell into narrowed dormancy preserves its existing bound/unbound/deleted evidence; it must never recapture a replacement parent. On candidate restoration, evidence invalidated by an actual deletion keeps a row dormant only if its reference/capture column is candidate-visible. An initially dangling nominal keep reference captures invalid=2 (unbound), distinct from invalid=1 (deleted captured lifetime); it may restore without inventing a lifetime. Deletion invalidates only bound (invalid=0) edges. Omitted reference cells and evidence stay retained for future restoration. A narrowed row never rebinds a deleted visible parent. Restored read-through images retain their historical captures and must pass the existing audience/deadline safety check. Whole-table restoration keeps the full source image through lifetime retirement and narrowed classification; ordinary candidate projection follows that boundary. Before projecting a narrowed image, evaluate retirement against its retained source definition/deadline, including direct expiry without read-through; removing read-through or expiry cannot revive an expired source lifetime. Actual retirement deletes the narrowed image, dependent captured evidence and its retained asset homes. Rows whose target table is absent stay dormant. Dormant rows run no jobs/expiry until reactivation. Assets remain retained but inaccessible. `prune --allow-destructive` drops narrowed images, associated dormant cells and lifetime evidence and releases only their asset homes; the preview names counts/bytes/sample ids. Inventory uses `rows:<table>` typed keys and audit items record before_rows, after_rows, restored_rows and bounded before_sample_ids in the activation transaction, including partial column/row restorations whose typed key remains. Per-table narrowed restoration counters distinguish restore-and-hide swaps with equal totals; summary restored_items includes partial restorations. Restoration identity sets are held transactionally and counted against final surviving r only after all read-through/cache activation phases. Column restored counts are recorded from cells actually consumed into surviving candidate rows, not inferred from inventory decreases; expiry/deletion may remove cells without restoring them. There is no automatic collection.

### 9.2 Exact preview and operator vocabulary

`rollback <dir> --to N --check` and `check --store <path> --release N` execute candidate adoption on a private SQLite snapshot created by VACUUM INTO through a read-only source connection. This is disk-backed, uses the exact adoption builder/validation, and never changes the live source, pointer, events or generation. SQLite supports VACUUM INTO on a read-only source; it acquires a coherent WAL read snapshot without the source writer lock. BUSY/disk failures refuse with retry advice, never fall back to copying sidecars. The temporary directory is owner-only and removed on success, refusal and ordinary unwinding. Process kill/crash may leave a private preview directory; operators can remove snapback4-release-preview-* leftovers when no preview uses them. The live format, including format 2, is never upgraded by preview. The snapshot may require disk/time proportional to the store; it provides a point-in-time plan, not a write freeze. The output enumerates the full candidate inventory and totals every narrowed population, not just the first sixteen status items; it includes any E_ADOPT refusal. Preview prepares the candidate native content identity but never starts workers or effects.

Deploy warns when type widening (including image/video byte bounds and file MIME/byte sets) admits values the previous release refuses; rollback also warns when its selected target admits values the current release refuses. All E_ADOPT inspect SQL uses quoted application table identifiers accepted by `sql`, including Quarry names that are SQL keywords; internal dormant inventory is inspected through `releases`. `latest` denotes the highest complete immutable source release, independently of current release and monotone generation. Complete here means a numeric directory with nonempty source that compiles; empty permanently reserved crash directories are skipped. Native preparation still validates a selected release before activation. Packaging and comparing the content digest precedes allocating a numeric release. Identical packaged source is a no-op: no new release directory, activation revision, audit or generation. A no-op is not an activation and therefore does not advance §5's activation revision. Widening warnings go to stderr, are advisory, and do not change a successful exit status. `releases <dir>` adds a JSON latest field; `releases <dir> [N|latest]` adds selected source id/digest/currentness without changing the authoritative current identity or activating; `rollback --to latest` and preview `--release latest` accept it. Command completion follows in-memory publication and prints `release N is current as generation G`.

### 9.3 HTTP admission and reporting
# Deploy: upgrade, real accounts and schema evolution

A release is immutable source over one durable store. Deploy from the app directory:

```sh
bunx snapback4 check
bunx snapback4 test
bunx snapback4 deploy ./deployment
bunx snapback4 serve ./deployment --port 4400
```

## Upgrade
`snapback4 upgrade [version]` resolves once and exactly pins core and installed Expo adapters with npm/bun/pnpm. Rebuild native apps after adapter changes; commit the lockfile, run check/typecheck/acceptance. `test` uses ./fixtures; `--fixture dir` overrides it. Use the password and first-admin recipes below.

## Deploy and password identity
Add `use identity password` (included below). Deploy from the app directory onto durable storage:

Serve admits bearer sessions; personas are 401 and /seed/dev doors 403, even through proxies. Signup creates an account, not a privileged role. See guide client for SDK session stores; auth/headless for account setup. HTTP auth returns session/denied; SDK signup/login from `snapback4/auth` returns ok/why. Check warns on installed version skew.

```sh
U=http://127.0.0.1:4400
curl -s "$U/auth/signup" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
# {session:{principal,kind,token,expiresAt,scope}}
curl -s "$U/auth/login" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
```

Keep the session and send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. Wrong credentials or a disabled identity method are 401 E_AUTH. Sessions survive ordinary deployments and restarts until expiry/revocation; a replaced/reset store requires sign-in again. Never delete a store to make adoption pass. `serve --port N` accepts 0 or A-B; stdout's first line is its URL, stderr includes SNAPBACK4_READY. Wait for readiness.

Layout: releases/N/ holds source; store.sqlite data/sessions/queues; assets/ uploads. The database owns current release/history; current is a text convenience copy, releases.jsonl a convenience ledger. Back up the whole directory, including sidecars/assets/releases, with serve and external workers stopped (CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §6). A restore does not rewind external effects.

## First admin: a service seat
Provision roles through an operator seat, with no public first-user race:
```quarry
use identity password
table admins:
  principal: principal
  unique principal
  immutable principal
  sync
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops' or exists admins[principal = viewer]
mutation grantAdmin(principal: principal):
  return insert admins { principal }
table notes:
  owner: principal
  body: text <=200
  archived: bool default false
  status: enum('draft', 'live') default 'draft'
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
```

```sh
TOKEN=$(bunx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment)
curl -s "$U/m/grantAdmin" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{"principal":"user:SIGNED_UP_ID"}}'
unset TOKEN
```

This roster is synced. A whole service-seat branch may add the first member; ordinary callers must already hold the roster. Service grants add no device audience. The seat's own program lookups and `exists` obey read rules too: to inspect a roster, add `or viewer = 'service:ops'` to its read rule, as above. A rule probe has authorization visibility; a program probe does not. The mutation completes before the grant syncs; wait for the enrolled account's client to receive its scope before using offline staff reads.

A synced row has at most one group-roster audience: cross-table crew grants are not a second group. Enroll those readers in the same roster or keep the table `online only` (`guide rule-recipes`).

Mint locally against the same store. Token stdout is only the credential; prose is stderr. Protect it; it expires under the seat-session policy. Rotate the credential by revoking its session (guide services) and minting a replacement. Expired or revoked bearer sessions cannot invoke the mutation. A repeat grant hits uniqueness; add membership revocation deliberately. Programmatic account sessions use `mintSession(url, serviceToken, principal)` from `snapback4/worker` (guide services); service seats themselves are minted locally by `token`.

## Migrations and adoption
Optional/defaulted columns, new tables/ops, non-unique indexes and wider types are additive. Required new fields need a literal default or explicit backfill; default now/viewer cannot invent historical values. Dev check/adoption refuses incompatible values with E_ADOPT. Release type narrowing instead retains each row whose present values cannot fit. Unique/cap conflicts still refuse atomically; repair active rows, or restore through a widened non-unique bridge, repair, then reinstate constraints. Prefer expand → migrate → contract for client compatibility.

## Rollback after widening, restore and prune
```sh
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1 --check
bunx snapback4 check --store ./deployment/store.sqlite --release 1
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1
bunx snapback4 releases ./deployment latest
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to latest
```

`deploy ./deployment --check` previews the deploy direction, including restoration intent.

Preview runs exact adoption on a private disk snapshot and removes it, without activating; disk/time scales with the store, and later writes can change the plan. Release is immutable source; latest is the highest successfully adopted source even after rollback. A refused candidate is never selectable. Bare rollback selects the previously active release, and generation a monotone identity; releases N/latest inspects source. Identical source is a no-op. Deploy/rollback return after serve switches: `release N is current as generation G`. In-flight one-shot operations finish in their generation; no settle sleep is needed. Default adoption is one measured receipt; --verbose/--json includes phases/cache advice. N_ADOPTION_PAUSE appears above one second. A dev/older unreachable owner requires stopping that owned process before retrying; failed adoption keeps current.

Widened enum values, or short codes a loose release wrote, become dormant rows when selected types cannot hold them; compatible forward restores exact values. Reference dependents also leave the active population. Rules, counts, maintained views and reacquired sync partitions see only active rows. Point reads/updates/ deletes see absent rows; dormant ids stay reserved. Nominal reference values stay legal, as for other missing ids; require/read-through decides live-target admission. Dormant rows occupy no cap/unique slots, so older writes can require a bridge repair. Removed tables/columns/indexes also stay dormant. Fresh-source name reuse needs --restore-dormant; rollback selects restoration explicitly. Old active updates keep dormant cells; active deletion removes them. Only active referential actions run: parent deletion invalidates dormant lifetimes. Narrowed rows with invalid visible references stay dormant; whole-table/column restoration retains its E_ADOPT barrier. Prune previews counts/bytes/sample ids; `prune ./deployment --allow-destructive` permanently drops all dormant storage. No age collector or implicit pruning exists. Deploy/rollback --allow-destructive bypasses neither validation nor retention. --allow-feed-adoption permits the explicit feed launch. Inspect releases after an ambiguous reply. Details: [SERVER.md — Release lifecycle](SERVER.md#release-lifecycle).

Check business compatibility separately: old payment code may accept payment while a new review row is dormant. Pause that workflow or deploy a bridge preserving its checks. Old source cannot supply absent product logic. An absent operation returns 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP",…},generation:G}`; a mutation's incompatible arguments return 200 `{state:"failed",why:{code:"E_INPUT",…},generation:G}`.

## What the old release sees
| Request after rollback | Result |
|---|---|
| An operation absent from the old release | HTTP 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP"}}` |
| Bare point query of a dormant row | Reads null |
| Point query or mutation with an explicit `! else CODE` guard | Refuses its guard code (`NOT_FOUND` when authored that way) |
| Added columns after rollback | Ignored by the older release; their cells stay dormant |
| Counts, rules, maintained views and sync | Only the active population |
| Compatible forward to the newer release | Original dormant values restored |

This proof assumes v1 declares `readItem(item)` returning `items[item]` and `editItem(item)` with `row = items[item]! else NOT_FOUND`; v2 adds `newOnly` and writes an enum value v1 cannot hold. Keep the bearer session in `headers`:
```js
await cli('rollback', deployment, '--to', '1');
const absent = await fetch(url + '/q/newOnly', {method:'POST', headers, body:'{"args":{}}'});
assert.equal(absent.status, 404);
assert.equal((await absent.json()).denied.code, 'E_OP');
assert.equal((await post('/m/editItem', {item})).why.code, 'NOT_FOUND');
assert.equal((await post('/q/readItem', {item})).data, null);
await cli('rollback', deployment, '--to', 'latest');
assert.equal((await post('/q/readItem', {item})).data.status, 'new');
```

Preview names missing operations, changed rule clauses, absent schedules and populated removed tables: dormant holds no longer enforce their safeguards. A changed job body is named separately; an unchanged job that compiles differently after a dependency change gets a note. Captures from the original read-through lifetime stay dormant with columns; restoring the same capture definition restores them. New rows without those captures still need safe audience correspondence.

Receipts and previews count storage by kind: `1 row, 2 cells, 0 index entries dormant`. Dormant rows cover their retained cells; standalone cell counts/samples belong only to active rows. Bytes/assets follow the same disjoint ownership. Samples are labelled and unique within each kind/path, for example `rows notes [A]; cells notes.color [B, C]`. A retained index definition represents logical entries to rebuild, not a dormant physical index. A widening note describes values later writes could use; a compatibility WARNING counts only current values the selected release cannot hold. Compatible forward has no such warning.

## Clients across rollback
Generated required fields and operations describe the schema checked when the client was built. Rolling the server back does not rebuild that client. Choose a release-matched frontend: in a separate copy of the selected immutable source, run `snapback4 check`, rebuild the frontend against that generated API and deploy it alongside the backend. Keep the immutable deployment source intact. An Expo/Exact runtime change also needs a device rebuild; JavaScript reload alone does not rebuild it.

For one frontend spanning both releases, author a compatibility boundary at the response: validate newer fields at runtime before using them, and render their older-release fallback. A v2 generated required `color` field can be absent under v1; TypeScript requiredness is no runtime guarantee. For example, `typeof row.color === 'string' ? row.color : 'none'` supplies the old-release fallback. Before exposing a newer workflow, check the authenticated served operation catalog (`GET /`) for its operation, and still handle `E_OP` if a switch races that check. Handle authored lookup guard refusals separately from bare-query null. Do not replace a missing safeguard with a frontend type assertion. Test both directions with the same frontend and bearer session, asserting field fallbacks, missing-operation handling, business checks and restored original values.

## Trigger a deployed job
A running serve accepts `POST /jobs/<job>` with a full service token; the job executes as the system on its FIFO queue and server clock. Every full service seat can trigger system jobs, so provision these tokens only to trusted operators. Guest/account sessions, read-scoped tokens and personas cannot use this door. `POST /dev/jobs/<job>` remains development-only.
```sh
export SNAPBACK4_SERVICE_TOKEN=$(bunx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment)
bunx snapback4 run reconcile --release ./deployment --json
unset SNAPBACK4_SERVICE_TOKEN
```
`--token-env NAME` reads another environment variable. The command uses served source, so it also works outside the app directory. A lost reply is ambiguous; inspect the queue before retrying. Starter/client recipes: `guide client`.

## Persona transition checklist
The complete account directory, linked membership and real-account transition is `guide production`.
1. Add password identity and signup/login UI; persist sessions with localStorageSessionStore on web or the secure native store, and verify restart.
2. Replace hard-coded persona owners/UI with signed-in account principals; map roles through the trusted seat recipe.
3. Give profiles a production write path and authorized directory; expose no credentials.
4. Create required teams/memberships, invitations or principal sharing; wait for scope delivery before offline reads; test expiry, revocation and outsiders.
5. Bootstrap every required table without fixtures/seed; guide start/client covers first writers and password starters.
6. Prove real-account writes, role checks, sign-out and rollback on durable storage behind TLS.

## Inspection, proxies and TLS
| Door | `dev` | `serve` |
|---|---|---|
| `/q`, `/m`, `GET /` compatibility record | Loopback persona or bearer | Bearer session required, including public rows |
| `/auth/config`; signup/login/guest | Public config; declared sign-in methods | Public config; declared sign-in methods |
| `/seed`, `/dev/*` | Development only | Refused |
| `/jobs/<job>` | Service seat | Full service seat |

Public visitors obtain guest sessions with `use identity`; `use identity password` disables guests (`guide rule-recipes`).

`snapback4 sql 'SELECT id, archived FROM notes' --release ./deployment --json` returns positional rows under `columns`; `--json-objects` returns object rows with direct boolean columns as true/false. Schema-qualified relations, recursive or shadowing/forward-reference CTEs need positional --json. Inspection bypasses read rules and can show expired stored rows; it is an operator tool, not an app query surface. Bind loopback behind a TLS proxy. Forward Authorization and preserve response bodies; allow long polls without buffering or early timeouts. Non-loopback HTTP requires `--i-understand-cleartext` and a trusted private hop behind TLS. Identity rate keys use the socket peer, not forwarded headers. Serve refuses personas and dev doors even through a local proxy; see SERVER.md for capacity. `--json-objects` returns `{header, columns, rows, limit, more, ruleFiltered:false}` with object `rows`; `--json` uses positional `rows`. Put flags outside the quoted SQL.

## Authoring and proof refusals
N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER means no production insert path can bootstrap this table. It groups root blockers with their dependents; persona-only schemas get one identity note. Add a service-seat insert mutation and provision real account relationships. E_UPGRADE means installation or post-upgrade check failed. Repair the package-manager or registry error and rerun upgrade. Packages may already be pinned: if check failed, repair the reported schema/device refusal and rerun check.

function state(path) {
  const db = new DatabaseSync(path, { readOnly: true });
  try {
    return {
      metadata: Object.fromEntries(db.prepare("SELECT key, value FROM _meta ORDER BY key").all().map(({ key, value }) => [key, value])),
      rows: db.prepare("SELECT t, id, data FROM r ORDER BY t, id").all(),
      indexes: db.prepare("SELECT ti, hex(k) AS key, id FROM i ORDER BY ti, k").all(),
      generations: db.prepare("SELECT generation, schema, programs FROM _generations ORDER BY generation").all(),
    };
  } finally { db.close(); }
}

//! Timings belong to a successful activation, never to an uncommitted candidate.
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

#[derive(Default, Debug)]
pub struct AdoptionReport {
    pub changes:Vec<String>,
    pub phases: Vec<(&'static str, Duration)>,
    /// Nested attribution, not additional time to add to `phases`.
    pub details: Vec<(String, Duration)>,
    pub views: Vec<ViewBuild>,
}

#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct ViewBuild {
    pub name: String,
    /// Source row visits; a row-keyed rebuild can visit one source per member.
    pub rows: u64,
    pub elapsed: Duration,
}

impl AdoptionReport {
    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#11-wave-11-factual-release-narration — current dormant storage is counted by unit; samples never mix units.
    pub fn dormant(items: &[super::release_history::DormantItem]) -> String {
        let mut counts = [0u64; 3];
        let mut samples = std::collections::BTreeMap::<String, std::collections::BTreeSet<String>>::new();
        for item in items {
            let (slot, label) = match item.kind.as_str() {
                "column" => (1, format!("cells {}", item.path)),
                "index" => (2, format!("entries {}", item.path)),
                _ => (0, format!("rows {}", item.path)),
            };
            counts[slot] += item.rows;
            if !item.sample_ids.is_empty() { samples.entry(label).or_default().extend(item.sample_ids.iter().cloned()); }
        }
        let labelled = samples.into_iter().map(|(label, ids)| format!("{label} [{}]", ids.into_iter().take(5).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        format!("{} {}, {} {}, {} index {} dormant{}", counts[0], if counts[0] == 1 { "row" } else { "rows" }, counts[1], if counts[1] == 1 { "cell" } else { "cells" }, counts[2], if counts[2] == 1 { "entry" } else { "entries" },
            if labelled.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!("; samples: {}", labelled.join("; ")) })
    }
    pub fn compact(&self)->String {
        let elapsed:Duration=self.phases.iter().map(|(_,d)|*d).sum();
        let mut changes=self.changes.iter().take(5).cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>();
        if self.changes.len()>5 {changes.push(format!("{} more changes",self.changes.len()-5));}
        format!("{}; {:.1} ms",if changes.is_empty(){"adopted".into()}else{changes.join(", ")},elapsed.as_secs_f64()*1000.0)
    }
    pub fn summary(&self) -> String {
        let report = self;
        let mut out = String::new();
        if !report.phases.is_empty() {
            out.push_str(&format!(
                "; phases [{}]",
                report
                    .phases
                    .iter()
                    .map(|(name, time)| format!("{name} {:.3} ms", time.as_secs_f64() * 1000.0))
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                    .join(", ")
            ));
        }
        if !report.details.is_empty() {
            out.push_str(&format!(
                "; phase details (nested) [{}]",
                report
                    .details
                    .iter()
                    .map(|(name, time)| format!("{name} {:.3} ms", time.as_secs_f64() * 1000.0))
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                    .join(", ")
            ));
        }
        if !report.views.is_empty() {
            out.push_str(&format!(
                "; rebuilt {}",
                report
                    .views
                    .iter()
                    .map(|view| format!(
                        "{} ({} source visits, {:.3} ms)",
                        view.name,
                        view.rows,
                        view.elapsed.as_secs_f64() * 1000.0
                    ))
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                    .join(", ")
            ));
        }
        out
    }

    pub(crate) fn detail(&mut self, name: impl Into<String>, start: Instant) {
        self.details.push((name.into(), start.elapsed()));
    }
    pub(crate) fn phase(&mut self, name: &'static str, start: &mut Instant) {
        self.phases.push((name, start.elapsed()));
        *start = Instant::now();
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007.001#3.11 — builder output names the parent field, never its private target.
pub(super) fn table_label(schema: &crate::Schema, name: &str) -> String {
    if let Some(maintain) = schema.maintain_for(name) {
        return maintain.label();
    }
    for (prefix, suffix) in [("$basis:", "live basis"), ("$recount:", "recount state")] {
        if let Some(target) = name.strip_prefix(prefix) {
            let target = schema
                .maintain_for(target)
                .map_or_else(|| target.to_owned(), |m| m.label());
            return format!("{target} {suffix}");
        }
    }
    name.to_owned()
}

#[test]
fn narr_counts_column_values_not_population_and_word_entries_not_rows() {
    let source = "table docs:\n  title: text <=100\n  status: enum('draft') default 'draft'\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  update <- allow\n  online only\n";
    let wide = source.replace("enum('draft')", "enum('draft', 'archived')").replace("  title: text", "  by search: words(title), id\n  title: text");
    let mut e = engine(&wide);
    for (id, title, status) in [("a", "red blue red", "archived"), ("b", "green", "draft"), ("c", "", "draft")] {
        seed(&mut e, "docs", serde_json::json!({"id":id,"title":title,"status":status}));
    }
    let (target, _) = compile(source);
    let (count, values) = e.store().rollback_incompatible_values(e.schema(), &target, "docs", "status").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(count, 1); assert_eq!(values, ["'archived'"]);
    activate(&mut e, source, 2).unwrap();
    let items = e.store_mut().dormant_inventory(None, usize::MAX).unwrap();
    let index = items.iter().find(|i| i.kind == "index" && i.path == "docs.search").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(index.rows, 3, "two distinct tokens plus one token; the narrowed image also contributes");
    assert_eq!(index.sample_ids, ["a", "b"]);
    assert_eq!(index.unit(), "entries");
    assert!(crate::store::adoption_report::AdoptionReport::dormant(&items).contains("1 row, 0 cells, 3 index entries dormant"));
}

#[test]
fn narr_text_constraints_and_literal_default_counts_share_activation_predicate() {
    let source = "table docs:\n  code: text <=10 default 'x'\n  public\n  online only\n";
    let mut e = engine(source);
    seed(&mut e, "docs", serde_json::json!({"id":"a","code":"long"}));
    seed(&mut e, "docs", serde_json::json!({"id":"b","code":"x"}));
    let (target, _) = compile(&source.replace("text <=10", "text 2..10").replace("default 'x'", "default 'xx'"));
    let (count, _) = e.store().rollback_incompatible_values(e.schema(), &target, "docs", "code").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(count, 1);
    let (same, _) = compile(source);
    assert_eq!(e.store().rollback_incompatible_values(e.schema(), &same, "docs", "code").unwrap().0, 0);
}

#[test]
fn narr_dormant_rows_cover_their_separately_retained_cells() {
    let mut e = engine(&v2());
    for id in ["a", "b", "c"] { seed(&mut e, "rows", serde_json::json!({"id":id,"n":1,"status":"active","extra":42})); }
    activate(&mut e, V1, 2).unwrap();
    let before = e.store_mut().dormant_inventory(None, usize::MAX).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(before.iter().filter(|i|i.kind == "column").map(|i|i.rows).sum::<u64>(), 3);
    activate(&mut e, "query value():\n  return 1\n", 3).unwrap();
    let after = e.store_mut().dormant_inventory(None, usize::MAX).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(after.iter().filter(|i|i.kind == "column").map(|i|i.rows).sum::<u64>(), 0);
    assert!(crate::store::adoption_report::AdoptionReport::dormant(&after).contains("3 rows, 0 cells"));
    let actual:i64 = e.store().conn().query_row("SELECT COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(id)+octet_length(data)),0) FROM _dormant_rows", [], |r|r.get(0)).unwrap();
    let cells: i64 = e.store().conn().query_row("SELECT COALESCE(SUM(octet_length(id)+octet_length(data)),0) FROM _dormant_values", [], |r|r.get(0)).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(after.iter().filter(|i|i.kind == "table").map(|i|i.bytes).sum::<u64>(), (actual + cells) as u64);
    assert!(after.iter().filter(|i|i.kind == "column").all(|i|i.bytes == 0 && i.sample_ids.is_empty()));
    activate(&mut e, &v2(), 1).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(e.store().count("rows").unwrap(), 3);
    assert!(e.store_mut().dormant_inventory(None, usize::MAX).unwrap().is_empty());
}

import { captureStdout } from './helpers/server-output.mjs';
import { waitFor, hangBudget } from './helpers/wait.mjs';
import { cleanup, withCleanup } from './helpers/cleanup.mjs';
// CLI activation and inspection over real persisted stores. Test processes
// and deployment directories are isolated and removed by their owner.
import test from "node:test";
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { spawn, spawnSync } from "node:child_process";
import { once } from "node:events";
import { chmodSync, existsSync, mkdtempSync, mkdirSync, readFileSync, readlinkSync, rmSync, symlinkSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
import { dirname, join } from "node:path";
import { DatabaseSync } from "node:sqlite";
import { setTimeout as delay } from "node:timers/promises";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { createLocalClient } from "../dist/local.js";
import { processDevice } from "../dist/native.js";

const root = join(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "../../..");
const binary = process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN ?? join(root, "target/debug/snapback4");
const source = `use identity guests
use personas alice
table items:
  body: text <=100
  read <- allow
  insert <- allow
  sync public
mutation add():
  return insert items {body: 'kept'}
query q():
  return 1
`;

function fixture(t) {
  const directory = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "sb4-cli-activation-"));
  t.after(() => rmSync(directory, { recursive: true, force: true }));
  const app = join(directory, "app"), deployment = join(directory, "deployment");
  mkdirSync(join(app, "snapback"), { recursive: true });
  const schema = join(app, "snapback/schema.q");
  writeFileSync(schema, source);
  const invoke = (...args) => spawnSync(binary, args, { cwd: app, encoding: "utf8", timeout: hangBudget() });
  const run = (...args) => {
    const result = invoke(...args);
    assert.equal(result.error, undefined, result.error?.message);
    assert.equal(result.status, 0, `${args.join(" ")}\n${result.stdout}\n${result.stderr}`);
    return result.stdout;
  };
  return { app, deployment, schema, invoke, run };
}

function state(path) {
  const db = new DatabaseSync(path, { readOnly: true });
  await serve(deployment, async (url, headers) => {
    run("deploy", deployment);
    let backend = await (await fetch(url + "/schema", { headers })).json();
    assert.equal(backend.generation, 2);
    assert.deepEqual(backend.programs.find((p) => p.name === "q").body, [{ Return: { Literal: { Int: 2 } } }]);
    run("rollback", deployment);
    backend = await (await fetch(url + "/schema", { headers })).json();
    assert.equal(backend.generation, 3);
    assert.deepEqual(backend.programs.find((p) => p.name === "q").body, [{ Return: { Literal: { Int: 1 } } }]);
  });
});

test("required pointer and ledger failures happen before database activation", { timeout: hangBudget(1) }, (t) => {
  const { deployment, schema, invoke, run } = fixture(t);
  run("deploy", deployment);
  const database = join(deployment, "store.sqlite"), before = state(database);
  writeFileSync(schema, source.replace("return 1", "return 2"));
  const pointer = join(deployment, "current");
  rmSync(pointer);
  mkdirSync(pointer);
  let result = invoke("deploy", deployment);
  assert.equal(result.error, undefined);
  assert.equal(result.status, 1);
  assert.match(result.stderr, /current must be a regular/);
  assert.deepEqual(state(database), before);
  rmSync(pointer, { recursive: true });
  writeFileSync(pointer, "1\n");
  const ledger = join(deployment, "releases.jsonl");
  rmSync(ledger);
  mkdirSync(ledger);
  result = invoke("deploy", deployment);
  assert.equal(result.error, undefined);
  assert.equal(result.status, 1);
  assert.deepEqual(state(database), before);
  assert.equal(readFileSync(pointer, "utf8"), "1\n");
});

        format!("{} {}, {} {}, {} index {} dormant{}", counts[0], if counts[0] == 1 { "row" } else { "rows" }, counts[1], if counts[1] == 1 { "cell" } else { "cells" }, counts[2], if counts[2] == 1 { "entry" } else { "entries" },

    }
    pub fn compact(&self)->String {
        let elapsed:Duration=self.phases.iter().map(|(_,d)|*d).sum();
        let mut changes=self.changes.iter().take(5).cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>();
        if self.changes.len()>5 {changes.push(format!("{} more changes",self.changes.len()-5));}
        format!("{}; {:.1} ms",if changes.is_empty(){"adopted".into()}else{changes.join(", ")},elapsed.as_secs_f64()*1000.0)
    }
    pub fn summary(&self) -> String {
        let report = self;
        let mut out = String::new();
        if !report.phases.is_empty() {
            out.push_str(&format!(
                "; phases [{}]",
                report
                    .phases
                    .iter()
                    .map(|(name, time)| format!("{name} {:.3} ms", time.as_secs_f64() * 1000.0))
                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                    .join(", ")
            ));
        }
        if !report.details.is_empty() {
            out.push_str(&format!(
                "; phase details (nested) [{}]",
                report
                    .details
                    .iter()
                    .map(|(name, time)| format!("{name} {:.3} ms", time.as_secs_f64() * 1000.0))

function state(path) {
  const db = new DatabaseSync(path, { readOnly: true });
  try {
    return {
      metadata: Object.fromEntries(db.prepare("SELECT key, value FROM _meta ORDER BY key").all().map(({ key, value }) => [key, value])),
      rows: db.prepare("SELECT t, id, data FROM r ORDER BY t, id").all(),
      indexes: db.prepare("SELECT ti, hex(k) AS key, id FROM i ORDER BY ti, k").all(),
      generations: db.prepare("SELECT generation, schema, programs FROM _generations ORDER BY generation").all(),
    };
  } finally { db.close(); }
}

async function serve(deployment, inspect) {
  const child = spawn(binary, ["serve", deployment, "--port", "0", "--bind", "127.0.0.1"], { stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"] });
  const readStdout = captureStdout(child);
  const exited = once(child, "exit");
  let log = "", url;
  child.stdout.on("data", (data) => { log += data; url ??= readStdout().match(/http:\/\/127\.0\.0\.1:\d+(?=\r?\n)/)?.[0]; });
  child.stderr.on("data", (data) => { log += data; });
  await withCleanup(async () => {
    const deadline = performance.now() + hangBudget();
    while (!url && child.exitCode == null && performance.now() < deadline) await delay(10);
    assert.ok(url, log || "release server did not listen");
    const anonymous = await fetch(url + "/schema", { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(hangBudget()) });
    assert.equal(anonymous.status, 401);
    assert.equal((await anonymous.json()).denied?.code, "E_AUTH");
    const signedIn = await (await fetch(url + "/auth/guest", { method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json" }, body: "{}" })).json();
    assert.ok(signedIn.session?.token, JSON.stringify(signedIn));
    await inspect(url, { authorization: `Bearer ${signedIn.session.token}` }, signedIn.session, () => log, child);
  }, async () => {
    if (child.exitCode == null && child.signalCode == null) child.kill("SIGTERM");

    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#11-wave-11-factual-release-narration — current dormant storage is counted by unit; samples never mix units.
    pub fn dormant(items: &[super::release_history::DormantItem]) -> String {
        let mut counts = [0u64; 3];
        let mut samples = std::collections::BTreeMap::<String, std::collections::BTreeSet<String>>::new();
        for item in items {
            let (slot, label) = match item.kind.as_str() {
                "column" => (1, format!("cells {}", item.path)),
                "index" => (2, format!("entries {}", item.path)),
                _ => (0, format!("rows {}", item.path)),
            };
            counts[slot] += item.rows;
            if !item.sample_ids.is_empty() { samples.entry(label).or_default().extend(item.sample_ids.iter().cloned()); }
        }
        let labelled = samples.into_iter().map(|(label, ids)| format!("{label} [{}]", ids.into_iter().take(5).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        format!("{} {}, {} {}, {} index {} dormant{}", counts[0], if counts[0] == 1 { "row" } else { "rows" }, counts[1], if counts[1] == 1 { "cell" } else { "cells" }, counts[2], if counts[2] == 1 { "entry" } else { "entries" },
            if labelled.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!("; samples: {}", labelled.join("; ")) })
    }

        let Some((t, id, data, definition)) = item else {
            break;
        };
        cursor = (t, id.clone());

        else {
            continue;
        };

or admission policy. Independent readiness reviews cover this section together
with LLP 3000.011.003.000 §11 and LLP 3000.001.000's NARR diagnostics.

Compatibility is directional. A narrowing warning counts active row images
whose named column value fails the candidate's same type/text/asset predicate
used in narrowed-row activation; it does not count table population or
reference-closed dependents. Literal defaults and optional nulls are materialized
under the current schema. Values are bounded, de-duplicated samples. Missing
required fields remain a backfill refusal, not a dormant-value warning.
Zero incompatible values produce a note without a population count. Deploy
widening describes values future writes could use and the previous release
that cannot hold them; forward selection of saved source emits no widening
warning. Counts describe the read snapshot at narration time; concurrent
writes can change a later activation. Preview still performs exact activation
on its coherent private snapshot and changes no live state.

## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and

## 1. Summary

LLP 2000 is right that the whole application is the unit of design, right
that ownership comes before abstraction, and right to ask for deterministic
simulation and an early real deployment. Its weakness is that it treats the
two structural choices that produced Snapback 2's costs as open research
questions, and then proposes a first experiment at exactly the scale where
those costs are invisible. This document takes a position on both, names
what to keep from Snapback 2 verbatim, and rewrites the first slice so it
can fail for the right reasons.

The position, in one paragraph. Snapback 2's *core* is excellent and should
be copied: the rule language and its uniformity analysis, the structural-fact
family, one data language, typed states with no silence, refusals that paste
a rewrite, personas, the successor classifier, the derived per-recipient
log, the loop verbs and their sub-second budgets. Two *substrates* under
that core are where the record shows the hours went, and both should be
flipped rather than re-investigated: (1) the static certificate should keep
certifying **authorization and shape** and stop pricing **bytes and steps**,
which move to a deterministic runtime meter with honest `capped`/abort
outcomes; and (2) the client should hold **a replica of the viewer's declared
partition** — the local-first shape every shipped sync engine and Snapback
2's own `deliver` log converge on — instead of a per-query result cache
proven by receipts. Those two flips dissolve most of the machinery that
leaked into the application (receipts, coverage, halos, validators, grants,
ID slices, drains, `withHistory`, follower inboxes, per-table retention
words) and turn the 28-second acquisition into a bulk stream whose floor is
the device's write speed. The first slice should be built on the **hub
fixture** (1,000 memberships, 500 follows, 100,000 messages), with the
day-two change designed before the day-one screen, a native client in the
first slice, and a deterministic simulator as the primary test harness.

Three claims this document makes that LLP 2000 does not:

1. **The costs have two named causes, and both are decisions, not
   mysteries.** Byte-priced static certification and the receipt-proven
   result cache account for every ergonomic residual in LLP 1018.008.009
   except documentation. §2 shows the mapping.
2. **The first experiment must include the hub.** Snapback 2 was "elegant on
   day one and hostile on day two" (LLP 1020) and broke at 1,000 memberships
   (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). A two-principal slice will pass and teach nothing.
3. **The process is part of the product.** Seventy-one LLPs, over two
   thousand commits, and seven ceiling raises in fourteen days produced a

## Summary

Snapback is a complete backend for application projects. It is designed to
**scale to zero** — to be so light that an idle app costs almost nothing and
needs no babysitting, letting you prototype without worry — while being built so
the same system can **scale up** to the workload of any realistically popular
production app.

It is **comprehensive and coherent**: it aims to give you everything a backend
needs under one roof, with the pieces designed to work together rather than
bolted on, a bit like Ruby on Rails. And it is **truly open** — MIT licensed,
with no source-available restrictions that would stop you from hosting it for
other people.

This document is the root of the Snapback LLP corpus. It captures the project's
intent, its subsystem boundaries, and the non-obvious constraints that every
follow-on design and every line of code is expected to respect. It reflects the
founder's stated direction for the project as of the date above. Treat the
direction here as active; treat subsystem details as boundary-level guidance
until follow-on LLPs turn them into implementation specs.

The corpus is flat under `llp/`. `llp/foundation/` links the kernel documents
from which the design could be recreated; `llp/current/` links the declared
working set. A document keeps its path for its whole life — `Tombstoned` and
`Superseded` are header statuses, not directories. `reviews/`, `current/`, and
`foundation/` are reserved names. Orient from `foundation/`, then `current/`,
then the `@ref`s in scope.

## Source and ratification

This document is based on Charlie Cheever's Snapback project brief from
2026-06-25 and the same-day review/revision pass over the README and LLP corpus.
Charlie is the ratifying source for the product direction captured here. Future
LLPs should preserve this distinction: this root document states the current
direction, while subsystem LLPs define the specific contracts agents implement.

A cross-family review of this document (Claude / Anthropic) was recorded under
[`llp/reviews/0000-snapback.claude.md`](./reviews/0000-snapback.claude.md) on
- The conformance corpus (`corpus/`) is Snapback 2's serialized IR and value
  model; the core keeps those serializations so it binds. A case the slice
  cannot run is skipped with a named reason, never counted as a pass.
- `snapback/generated/api.ts` is built by `check` and `dev`, never committed;
  the app's typecheck needs a `check` first (the suite does one).
- Point rule probes and `exists` require a unique key. A fully pinned superset
  of a required unique key is unique too (for example an explicit role index
  containing the unique conversation/principal pair). Nullable unique key
  arguments need non-null proof. Other existence checks use bounded populations
  in code. Named equalities covering a total unique key check extra columns on
  that one row without an additional index.
- The packaged CLI uses `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT` during both publication tests and its production build (LLP 3000 §7). A plain production Cargo build retains the dependency's ordinary SQLite policy; use the same build input when comparing with the package. The variable is unnecessary at runtime.
- When creating a stage, `scripts/publish.mjs` runs the suite and refuses on red, builds the production binary, packs `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and verifies the installed digest, then publishes both packages. Bump `version` in `packages/snapback4/package.json` (and its `optionalDependencies`), `packages/snapback4-darwin-arm64/package.json`,
  `packages/snapback4-linux-x64/package.json` and `Cargo.toml` together; a test refuses a mismatch.
- Publication retains exact qualified tarballs under `target/publication/`. If a
  stage exists, use `node scripts/publish.mjs --resume` to validate its recorded
  suite qualification, requalify those exact bytes through the installed-consumer
  proof, and finish missing registry artifacts; an existing version must match
  its staged integrity. This also applies after `--dry-run`. An ordinary invocation
  refuses an existing stage so changed source cannot silently publish old bytes.
  Bump the version to build a new pair; preserve a partially published stage.
- Cross-host publication (LLP 3000.021 §3.3): prepare the common client once
  from clean release source, qualify that tarball on Linux, and import the
  Linux tarball plus its receipt on the Mac. Source commit, client SRI and
  Linux SRI must agree. Linux prefers working Docker/Podman, otherwise uses checksum-pinned proot
  with clean Ubuntu 24.04/Alpine root filesystems in a user cache, and refuses
  `NPM_TOKEN` or `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`, even when empty. It never signs or packs
  the client. The Mac never rebuilds or executes Linux; `--resume` rechecks
  its copied receipt. No receipt packs a Darwin-only client; an existing
  two-platform preparation is refused on that path. Packed dependencies must
  match the staged set. Uploads use verified private snapshots, with each
  digest checked again immediately before publish.
  Commands and current qualification limits:
  [`reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md`](./reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md).
- The guide is tested text. Every ```quarry block in `crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs` and in `packages/snapback4/README.md` is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs` with no diagnostic and no note (the language card is also run against the engine); every refusal code the sources emit must be in `guide::CODES`; every `guide("..")` a diagnostic carries must name a card. Add a form to the language card when you add it to the parser, fence README Quarry as ```quarry, and never fence Quarry as plain text to dodge the test.

fn compile(source: &str) -> (Schema, Vec<crate::ir::Program>) {
    let b = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    (
        serde_json::from_str(&serde_json::to_string(&b.schema).unwrap()).unwrap(),
        b.programs
            .into_iter()
            .map(|p| serde_json::from_str(&serde_json::to_string(&p.program).unwrap()).unwrap())
            .collect(),
    )
}
fn engine(source: &str) -> Engine {
    let (s, p) = compile(source);
    Engine::new_release(Store::memory().unwrap(), s, p, 1, None).unwrap()
}
fn activate(e: &mut Engine, source: &str, n: u64) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
    let (s, p) = compile(source);
    let previous = e
        .store()
        .meta("release")
        .unwrap()
        .and_then(|s| s.parse().ok());
    e.adopt_release(s, p, &ReleaseActivation::new(n, previous), false)
}
fn seed(e: &mut Engine, name: &str, json: serde_json::Value) {
    let row = wire::row_from_json(&e.schema().tables[name], &json).unwrap();
    e.seed([(name.into(), row)]).unwrap();
}

 ## 1. Commission and scope
@@ -70,6 +70,13 @@ The server continues an admitted activation even if the client hits its 120-seco
 
 ### 5.1 Path-independent control address
 
+The accept loop polls a nonblocking listener, but immediately resets each
+accepted stream to blocking mode before peer validation or input. Darwin/BSD
+inherits the listener's nonblocking flag; Linux does not. Blocking streams
+let the existing total input deadline govern clients that write after accept.
+If resetting the stream fails, close it and report the accept error; never
+admit a command on a stream with unknown I/O mode.
+
 Amendment commissioned and implemented by Codex for Charlie on 2026-10-12,
 for the 0.4.24 macOS publication blocker. Darwin's Unix socket path limit
 is 104 bytes; the previous deployment-local name failed under ordinary

 covered cells, independently of the current inventory counts.

**READY — no material concern remains in PUBFIX28 change 1.** Reviewer: Codex (GPT-6), independently reviewing the worktree against `8fe2b2e839`, including FIX28 `4e96153fdf`.

The **old test assertion should change**. Its sole row has status `new`, incompatible with release 1. That dormant whole row covers its twenty retained cells, so `1 row, 0 cells, 0 index entries dormant` is correct. Adding the compatible row correctly yields `1 row, 20 cells`; each column’s JSON samples contain only that live row’s id.

The revised prose avoids the contradictory zero-cell claim. The current regression covers all twenty columns, empty JSON samples, mixed populations, preview state preservation, and forward restoration using the stored tagged value format. Snapshot adoption remains isolated from the live database. §11 consistently documents the existing disjoint inventory rule and these fixtures, with sufficient evidence for semantic readiness.
